    27	/**
    28	 * One screen of a Read, Recall, Colour SECTION run.
    29	 *
    30	 * Presentational, like `RecallStage`, and separate from it because the rule it
    31	 * keeps is different. v1 hides one chunk during its recall; a section has two
    32	 * looks and nothing between them: the whole stimulus while she works it, and
    33	 * no stimulus at all during The Recall
    34	 * (`docs/adr/0013-she-chunks-freely-and-the-app-does-not-track-it.md`).
    35	 *
    36	 * **It wears the real section's clothes.** The drill she is training for is sat
    37	 * on LawHub, and `OfficialDrillFrame` already mirrors that surface: indigo
    38	 * field, white header rail, one centred white paper card, the stimulus in
    39	 * Fraunces at 19px. This screen replicates that contract exactly and removes
    40	 * the answering half -- no question column, no choices, no verdict, no
    41	 * notepad -- so the drill feels like the thing it is preparing her for rather
    42	 * than like a separate app. The frame itself is not imported because it carries
    43	 * auth, the notepad and the tutor toggle, none of which may exist inside this
    44	 * loop, and because this component has to stay presentational.
    45	 *
    46	 * The hard requirements, all of them negative:
    47	 *
    48	 * - **No place marker.** The app does not track which chunk she is on, so no
    49	 *   chunk is styled as current, done, or upcoming. She reads, recalls and
    50	 *   colours in whatever units she likes.
    51	 * - **The text truly vanishes for The Recall.** Not blurred, not transparent,
    52	 *   not aria-hidden: removed. A stimulus left in the DOM would be read back to
    53	 *   a student using a screen reader, which defeats the drill for her exactly.
    54	 * - **One key.** No mouse in the loop. The only control here is Space, wired
    55	 *   by the runner; the exit is outside the loop.
    56	 * - **No answer choices, and nothing judged.** Nothing is scored, shown or
    57	 *   corrected until the section ends, because a live verdict trains her to vet
    58	 *   before speaking, which is the habit colouring exists to break.
    59	 *
    60	 * The clock counts up rather than down, and the microphone has one quiet
    61	 * indicator beside the instruction. Both are late additions and both are
    62	 * concessions to the same finding: a student cannot pace a 25-minute section
    63	 * she cannot see the time on, and she cannot tell a dead microphone from her
    64	 * own silence. Neither may ever read as a score.
    65	 */

   171	export function SectionStage({

   197	  return (

   201	      <header className="ac-section-head">

   204	        </span>

   206	          <span className="ac-section-rail-fill" style={{ transform: `scaleX(${through})` }} />

   213	          * drill is against the clock, though, and a student with no way to see
   214	          * the time cannot pace a 25-minute section. Counting up rather than

import { useCallback, useEffect, useMemo, useRef, useState } from "react";
import { supabase } from "@/integrations/supabase/client";
import type { Database } from "@/integrations/supabase/types";
import { completeSectionRun, requestSectionGrading } from "../api/activitiesClient";
import { SectionStage } from "./SectionStage";
import { coachNote, hasSeenWalkthrough, markWalkthroughSeen } from "./coach";
import { useSectionRecorder, type RecordingSegment } from "./useSectionRecorder";
import { sliceTranscript, useSectionTranscript } from "./useSectionTranscript";
import { beginBackup, discard, putChunk, stashRows } from "./recordingBackup";
import { beginLiveRun } from "@/lib/liveRun";
import {
  advanceCursor,
  buildSectionRun,
  startCursor,
  type Accommodation,
  type SectionCursor,
} from "./section";
import "./section.css";

/**
 * The generated insert shape for one attempt row, plus `rrc`.
 *
 * Same intersection, and the same reason, as `RecallRunner`: the column was
 * added by `20260829140000_recall_responses.sql` and `types.ts` is generated
 * from the deployed schema, so it will only learn the column the next time
 * types are pulled against a database that has the migration applied. Delete
 * the intersection once they are.
 */
type ResponseInsert = Database["public"]["Tables"]["activity_item_responses"]["Insert"];

/**
 * Where a section's recording is kept. Private, and readable only by the
 * student it belongs to and the instructor; see the bucket's own migration.
 */
const RECORDING_BUCKET = "drill-recordings";

/**
 * A Read, Recall, Colour SECTION run: 22 to 26 stimuli, end to end, one clock,
 * no stopping.
 *
 * Two rules shape everything here, both from
 * `docs/specs/2026-08-31-rrc-section-mode.md` section 4, and both negative:
 *
 * - **No coach in the loop.** Nothing is judged, shown or scored until the
 *   section ends. A live verdict trains her to vet before speaking, which is
 *   the exact habit colouring exists to break.
 * - **Nothing the loop waits on.** A dropped connection must not be able to
 *   stall the student mid-section. This used to be enforced by writing nothing
 *   at all until the last stimulus, which held for twenty minutes of work in
 *   the tab's memory and lost every second of it to a crash, a closed lid or a
 *   flat battery. The rule it was protecting is that the loop must not WAIT,
 *   not that nothing may be written -- so each stimulus is now handed to the
 *   database as it finishes, fired and forgotten, from an effect rather than
 *   from the keypress. Nothing is awaited between one stimulus and the next,
 *   and a write that fails is picked up by the backstop at the end.
 *
 *   A section is still the unit of work: half a section is not a partial score
 *   and never becomes one. That is enforced where it belongs, in the grader,
 *   which grades a run only once it is marked finished. Saving and judging are
 *   different acts, and conflating them is what cost the twenty minutes.
 *
 * It records. One unbroken file for the whole section, started before the loop
 * and stopped after it, exactly as the drill requires, uploaded once at the end
 * to a private bucket only she and the instructor can read. Every phase
 * boundary is stamped as an offset into that file, so each portion of each
 * stimulus is addressable without the recorder ever having been paused. See
 * `useSectionRecorder`.
 *
 * A refused or missing microphone does not stop the drill. It says so and the
 * section continues, because the drill is still a valid drill run against a
 * handheld recorder, and twenty minutes is far too long to spend talking into
 * nothing without being told.
 */

export interface SectionRunnerProps {
  assignmentId: string;
  studentId: string;
  /** The assignment's snapshot: items, the clock, and which section it is. */
  items: unknown;
  sourceSection: string | null | undefined;
  timeGoalMinutes: unknown;
  /**
   * Which goal her time is scored against. Defaults to standard; a student with
   * 53% or 70% extra time is not slow when she takes longer, and grading her
   * against the standard goal would score her accommodation as a failure.
   */
  accommodation?: Accommodation;
  onComplete?: () => void;
  onExit?: () => void;
  /** Injected in tests. Real runs use the wall clock. */
  now?: () => number;
  /**
   * The ready beat, in seconds. Injected in tests so a run can be driven
   * without waiting out three real seconds; every real run takes the default.
   */
  readySeconds?: number;
}

/**
 * What one stimulus's run looked like, written once the section is over.
 *
 * Stimulus-grained, like the cursor: time with the text up, time recalling
 * from memory, and nothing per chunk, because no chunk boundary was observed
 * (`docs/adr/0013-she-chunks-freely-and-the-app-does-not-track-it.md`).
 */
interface StimulusRecord {
  slug: string;
  order: number;
  /** Seconds from the section's start to the end of this stimulus. */
  finishedAtSeconds: number;
  /** Seconds spent on this stimulus alone. */
  elapsedSeconds: number;
  /** Seconds with the text on screen: her reads, recalls and colouring. */
  workingSeconds: number;
  /** Seconds spent giving The Recall, text gone. */
  recallSeconds: number;
}

/** Stable identity so hooks keyed on `now` do not restart every render. */
const defaultNow = () => Date.now();

/**
 * How long the ready beat runs before the first stimulus.
 *
 * Long enough to read one line and see the level move, short enough that it is
 * never in the way of a student on her fourth section of the week.
 */
const READY_SECONDS = 3;

/** A row gets a few quiet chances while she is still working, never an unbounded loop. */
const ROW_RETRY_ATTEMPTS = 3;
const ROW_RETRY_BASE_MS = 500;
const ROW_RETRY_MAX_MS = 4_000;

export function SectionRunner({
  assignmentId,
  studentId,
  items,
  sourceSection,
  timeGoalMinutes,
  accommodation = "standard",
  onComplete,
  onExit,
  now = defaultNow,
  readySeconds = READY_SECONDS,
}: SectionRunnerProps) {
  const run = useMemo(
    () => buildSectionRun(items, sourceSection, timeGoalMinutes),
    [items, sourceSection, timeGoalMinutes],
  );
  if (!run) {
    return (
      <div className="ac-section">
        <div className="ac-section-body">
          <p className="ac-section-instruction">This section could not be read</p>
          {onExit ? (
            <button type="button" className="ac-section-exit" onClick={onExit}>
              Leave
            </button>
          ) : null}
        </div>
      </div>
    );
  }
  return (
    <RunBody
      run={run}
      assignmentId={assignmentId}
      studentId={studentId}
      accommodation={accommodation}
      onComplete={onComplete}
      onExit={onExit}
      now={now}
      readySeconds={readySeconds}
    />
  );
}

function RunBody({
  run,
  assignmentId,
  studentId,

  onExit,

  readySeconds,
}: {

  const [coachOn, setCoachOn] = useState(() => !hasSeenWalkthrough(studentId));

   * one, with no moment in between that said the recording had begun. Three
   * seconds is the whole fix: the recorder is live through it, so the level
   * indicator is moving and a dead microphone is visible BEFORE any of it
   * costs her anything, and the section's own clock does not start until it
   * ends.
   */
  const [countdown, setCountdown] = useState<number | null>(readySeconds);
  /** Confirming the way out. Leaving throws the whole run away. */
  const [confirmingExit, setConfirmingExit] = useState(false);
  /**
   * Nothing reaching the microphone. Shown rather than logged: it is the one
   * fault that otherwise ends in a perfect-looking run and a card of zeros.
   *
   * Two-way, and it was not. `setDeadInput(true)` was the only call in the
   * tree, so the warning could be raised and never lowered: one skipped Recall

   * there for the remaining twenty-five stimuli, through four minutes of speech
   * the drill heard perfectly well. `VoiceLevel` now reports the state rather
   * than announcing the fault, and this is the setter for both directions.
   */
  const [deadInput, setDeadInput] = useState(false);

  const [deadInput, setDeadInput] = useState(false);
  /** The recording did not reach the server. Said plainly at the end. */
  const [uploadFailed, setUploadFailed] = useState(false);

   * filed under something. The run record that says where the pieces belong is
   * written as soon as the Attempt is known.
   */

    typeof crypto !== "undefined" && "randomUUID" in crypto
      ? crypto.randomUUID()

  } = useSectionRecorder(now, keepChunk);
  /**

   * again and no way to stop before the clock started.

   * last frame forever. A ref changes nothing about when the effect runs.

  const startedAt = useRef(now());

  const phaseStartedAt = useRef(now());
  const stimulusStartedAt = useRef(now());

      const segments = segmentsByOrder.current.get(r.order) ?? [];

        .select("attempt_no")
        .eq("assignment_id", assignmentId)
        .eq("student_id", studentId)
        .order("attempt_no", { ascending: false })
        .limit(1);
      // A failed read must not stop the drill. Attempt 1 is the honest guess
      // when nothing is known, and a collision on it is caught by the unique
      // index rather than by overwriting anything.

      if (cancelled) return;

}

   122	  if (!navigator.mediaDevices?.getUserMedia) throw new Error("no microphone on this device");

   211	     * which is what the bias buys, and why it is clamped to the start of the
   212	     * run rather than allowed to reach behind it and lose her first words.
   213	     */
   214	    const at = Math.max(timeline[0].startMs, piece.atMs - graceMs);
   215	    let chosen: { startMs: number; endMs: number } | null = null;
   216	    for (const seg of timeline) {
   217	      if (seg.startMs > at) break;
   218	      chosen = seg;
   219	    }
   220	    if (!chosen) continue;
   221	    // Past the end of the last window is past the end of the run.
   222	    if (at >= chosen.endMs && chosen === timeline[timeline.length - 1]) continue;
   223	    const i = segments.indexOf(chosen);
   224	    if (i >= 0) out[i].push(piece.text);
   225	  }
   226	  return out.map((texts) => texts.join(" ").trim());

  const handleKeyDown = (e: React.KeyboardEvent<HTMLTextAreaElement>) => {
    if (templatePaletteOpen && filteredTemplates.length > 0) {
      if (e.key === 'ArrowDown') {
        e.preventDefault();
        setTemplatePaletteIndex((index) => (index + 1) % filteredTemplates.length);
        return;
      }
      if (e.key === 'ArrowUp') {
        e.preventDefault();
        setTemplatePaletteIndex((index) => (index - 1 + filteredTemplates.length) % filteredTemplates.length);
        return;
      }
      if (e.key === 'Tab' || (e.key === 'Enter' && !e.metaKey && !e.ctrlKey)) {
        e.preventDefault();
        insertTemplate(filteredTemplates[templatePaletteIndex]);
        return;
      }
      if (e.key === 'Escape') {
        e.preventDefault();
        setTemplatePaletteOpen(false);
        return;
      }
    }
    // Enter sends; Shift+Enter inserts a newline. Cmd/Ctrl+Enter still sends
    // for muscle memory.
    if (e.key === 'Enter' && !e.shiftKey) {
      e.preventDefault();
      send();
    }
  };

  return (

import type { RecentSectionRun } from "../api/activitiesClient";

/**
 * What the launch surface says about a student's last few runs, derived and
 * nothing else.
 *
 * Every number here is read off runs she has already done. Nothing is scored,
 * nothing is predicted, and a number that cannot be established honestly is
 * absent rather than estimated -- which is why several of these are nullable.
 * The rule the whole file obeys: if we cannot know it, we do not print it.
 */

/**
 * One stimulus slot in a run, as the tick row draws it.
 *
 * Three states, not two, and the third is the important one. `notJudged` is a
 * stimulus she recorded and the judge returned no verdict for: our outage, not
 * her miss. Drawing it as an empty slot would tell her she skipped work she
 * actually did.
 *
 * `silent` is the fourth, and it is the one that is easy to get wrong. A
 * stimulus she never spoke into still produces a full grade row: the grader
 * writes `spoken: ""` with zero coverage and NO `ungraded` flag
 * (`grade-rrc-section/index.ts`). Treating the presence of a row as evidence of
 * recall would count her silence as work done and report "25 of 25" to somebody
 * who said nothing.
 *
 * The names deliberately avoid `carried`, which already means something else on
 * `SectionStimulusGrade` -- the NUMBER of details retained. Reusing it for "a
 * verdict exists" would make the two readings collide.
 */
export type TickState = "recorded" | "notJudged" | "silent" | "missing";

export interface RunTicks {
  /** One entry per authored slot, in authored order. */
  ticks: TickState[];
  /** Stimuli she actually spoke into, judged or not. Silence is not counted. */
  recorded: number;
  /** Authored slots in the section. */
  slots: number;
}

export interface SectionStats {
  /** Seconds on the clock for the most recent run. */
  lastRunSeconds: number | null;
  /**
   * Newest minus oldest, in seconds, across the runs shown. Negative is faster.
   * Null with fewer than two runs, where there is no trend to state.
   */
  trendSeconds: number | null;
  /** How many runs the trend spans. */
  trendRuns: number;
  /** Stimuli recorded across every run whose slot count is knowable. */
  recalled: number | null;
  /** Authored slots across those same runs. */
  recalledOf: number | null;
  /** The clock this section is run against, from the assignment. */
  targetMinutes: number | null;
  /** Runs finished inside the clock, of the ones measured against THIS target. */
  metTarget: number;

import { render, screen } from "@testing-library/react";
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { SectionStage } from "./SectionStage";
import type { SectionStimulus } from "./section";

/*
 * The footer's right-hand slot was a ternary, so a microphone warning did not
 * sit beside the key hint -- it replaced it. On the one run where something has
 * already gone wrong, the only line telling her which key advances the drill
 * disappeared.
 */

const stimulus: SectionStimulus = {
  slug: "pt-134-s3-q07",
  chunks: [{ n: 1, text: "The maintenance logs do not establish what the curators assumed." }],
} as SectionStimulus;

function renderStage(error: string | null) {
  return render(
    <SectionStage
      stimulus={stimulus}
      phase="working"
      stimulusNumber={7}
      totalStimuli={26}
      error={error}
      elapsedSeconds={461}
    />,
  );
}

describe("the drill's footer under a fault", () => {
  it("keeps the key hint on screen while the warning is up", () => {
    renderStage("Nothing is reaching the microphone. Check it is not muted, then keep going.");
    expect(screen.getByText(/nothing is reaching the microphone/i)).toBeInTheDocument();
    expect(screen.getByText("space")).toBeInTheDocument();
    expect(screen.getByText(/recall full stimulus/i)).toBeInTheDocument();
  });

  it("shows the hint alone when there is no fault", () => {
    renderStage(null);
    expect(screen.getByText("space")).toBeInTheDocument();
    expect(screen.queryByText(/nothing is reaching the microphone/i)).toBeNull();
  });
});
import { render, screen } from "@testing-library/react";
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { SectionStage } from "./SectionStage";
import type { SectionStimulus } from "./section";

/*
 * The one thing she asked to be able to see mid-run: that it is recording.
 * A word in the rail, not only a red dot, so it survives grayscale and a
 * screen reader. Absent, not merely hidden, when nothing is being recorded.
 */

const stimulus: SectionStimulus = {
  slug: "pt-134-s3-q07",
  chunks: [{ n: 1, text: "The maintenance logs do not establish what the curators assumed." }],
} as SectionStimulus;

function renderStage(recording: boolean) {
  return render(
    <SectionStage
      stimulus={stimulus}
      phase="working"
      stimulusNumber={7}
      totalStimuli={26}
      error={null}
      elapsedSeconds={461}
      recording={recording}
    />,
  );
}

describe("the recording mark in the rail", () => {
  it("says Recording, in words, while the microphone is open", () => {
    renderStage(true);
    const mark = document.querySelector(".ac-section-rec");
    expect(mark).not.toBeNull();
    expect(mark?.getAttribute("role")).toBe("status");
    expect(mark?.textContent).toMatch(/recording/i);
  });

  it("says nothing when nothing is being recorded", () => {
    renderStage(false);
    expect(screen.queryByText(/^recording$/i)).toBeNull();
  });
});
=== transcript test
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { sliceTranscript, type TranscriptPiece } from "./useSectionTranscript";

// Cutting the transcript at the recording's own boundaries.
//
// This is where the text channel and the audio channel are made to agree. If
// they disagree, a scorer reads her recall of one line as her Read of another,
// which is a wrong grade that looks like a right one.

const piece = (atMs: number, text: string): TranscriptPiece => ({ atMs, text });

describe("cutting her words at the same places as the audio", () => {
  it("files each piece under the segment it was spoken in", () => {
    const pieces = [piece(500, "first"), piece(2500, "second"), piece(4500, "third")];
    const segments = [
      { startMs: 0, endMs: 1000 },
      { startMs: 1000, endMs: 3000 },
      { startMs: 3000, endMs: 5000 },
    ];
    expect(sliceTranscript(pieces, segments)).toEqual(["first", "second", "third"]);
  });

  it("joins everything said inside one segment, in order", () => {
    const pieces = [piece(100, "say it"), piece(400, "back"), piece(700, "in my own words")];
    expect(sliceTranscript(pieces, [{ startMs: 0, endMs: 1000 }])).toEqual([
      "say it back in my own words",
    ]);
  });

  it("keeps a late final result with the turn it belongs to", () => {
    // Recognition settles a final result after the words were spoken, so her
    // last few words land just past the boundary. Filing them under the next
    // segment would put the end of her colour inside a silent read.
    const pieces = [piece(1050, "what if we are not Ireland")];
    const segments = [
      { startMs: 0, endMs: 1000 },
      { startMs: 1000, endMs: 2000 },
    ];
    expect(sliceTranscript(pieces, segments)).toEqual(["what if we are not Ireland", ""]);
  });

  it("returns an empty string for a segment she said nothing in", () => {
    // A silent read is the ordinary case, not a fault, and the scorer has to be
    // able to tell it apart from a recall she skipped.
    const segments = [
      { startMs: 0, endMs: 1000 },
      { startMs: 1000, endMs: 2000 },
    ];
    expect(sliceTranscript([piece(1500, "only here")], segments)).toEqual(["", "only here"]);
  });

  it("returns one empty string per segment when nothing was captured at all", () => {
    // No recognition in this browser. Every segment is blank, and blank has to
    // mean "not captured" rather than "she was silent".
    const segments = [
      { startMs: 0, endMs: 1000 },
      { startMs: 1000, endMs: 2000 },
    ];
    expect(sliceTranscript([], segments)).toEqual(["", ""]);
  });

Tool result:
 * the wrong default even before it stopped working. The drill is spoken end to
 * end and the grader refuses a run with no recording, so the ready beat now
 * holds rather than counting down into twenty-five minutes nothing can mark.
 * The beat holding without a microphone is asserted on its own, below.
 */
class FakeRecorder {
  static isTypeSupported = () => true;
  ondataavailable: ((e: { data: Blob }) => void) | null = null;
  onstop: (() => void) | null = null;
  state = "inactive";
  constructor(
    _stream: MediaStream,
    readonly options?: { mimeType?: string },
  ) {}
  get mimeType() {
    return this.options?.mimeType ?? "audio/webm";
  }
  start() {
    this.state = "recording";
  }
  stop() {
    this.state = "inactive";
    this.ondataavailable?.({ data: new Blob(["x"], { type: this.mimeType }) });
    this.onstop?.();
  }
}

/** Granted, unless a test says otherwise. */
function grantMicrophone() {
  const stream = { getTracks: () => [{ stop: () => {} }] } as unknown as MediaStream;
  vi.stubGlobal("MediaRecorder", FakeRecorder);
  Object.defineProperty(navigator, "mediaDevices", {
    configurable: true,
    value: { getUserMedia: vi.fn(() => Promise.resolve(stream)) },
  });
}

/** Refused, the way a browser refuses it. */
function refuseMicrophone() {
  vi.stubGlobal("MediaRecorder", FakeRecorder);
  const denied = Object.assign(new Error("denied"), { name: "NotAllowedError" });
  Object.defineProperty(navigator, "mediaDevices", {
    configurable: true,
    value: { getUserMedia: vi.fn(() => Promise.reject(denied)) },
  });
}

/** One stimulus, so a full run is two presses. */
const ONE = { ...pt105, items: [pt105.items[0]] };
/** Two, for the rules about what never comes back. */
const TWO = { ...pt105, items: pt105.items.slice(0, 2) };
/** The 26-stimulus section observed in the audit. */
const TWENTY_SIX = pt134;
const CHUNKS = ONE.items[0].chunks;

async function renderRun(overrides: Partial<Parameters<typeof SectionRunner>[0]> = {}) {
  const onComplete = vi.fn();
  const view = render(
    <SectionRunner
      assignmentId="a1"
      studentId="s1"
      items={ONE.items}
      sourceSection={ONE.source_section}
      timeGoalMinutes={ONE.time_goal_minutes}
      onComplete={onComplete}
      // The three-second ready beat is real and tested on its own, below. Every
      // other test here is about the loop AFTER it, so they start where a
      // student starts pressing rather than waiting three seconds each.
      readySeconds={0}
      {...overrides}
    />,
  );
  // The microphone is asked for on mount and answers in a microtask. The ready
  // beat waits for that answer now, so a test that pressed Space before it
  // arrived would be pressing at a held beat rather than at stimulus one.
  await act(async () => {});
  return { ...view, onComplete };
}

const space = () => fireEvent.keyDown(document, { code: "Space" });
const finish = (count: number) => {
  for (let i = 0; i < count * 2; i += 1) space();
};

Tool result:
.ac-section {
  --sec-field: #1a1635;
  --sec-paper: #ffffff;
  --sec-ink: #111827;
  --sec-rule: #cbd5e1;
  --sec-chrome: #334155;
  --sec-warn: #b45309;
  --sec-sans: "Helvetica Neue", Helvetica, Arial, sans-serif;

  display: flex;
  min-height: 100vh;
  flex-direction: column;
  background: var(--sec-field);
  font-family: var(--sec-sans);
}

/* ---- the white rail ---------------------------------------------------- */

/* Four children, so four columns: position, the rail, the clock, the way out.
   Declared as three, the exit wrapped onto a second row and the rail grew the
   header from 46px to 96px on the drill's only screen. */
.ac-section-head {
  display: grid;
  grid-template-columns: auto 1fr auto auto auto;
  align-items: center;
  gap: 1.25rem;
  padding: 0.75rem 1.5rem;
  border-bottom: 1px solid #e2e8f0;
  background: var(--sec-paper);
  color: var(--sec-chrome);
  font-size: 0.8125rem;
}

/* Where she is in the section. The clock sits beside it; see
   `.ac-section-clock` for why it counts up rather than down. */
.ac-section-where {
  font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums;
}

.ac-section-where b {
  font-weight: 600;
  color: var(--sec-ink);
}

.ac-section-rail {
  height: 2px;
  overflow: hidden;
  border-radius: 9999px;
  background: #e2e8f0;
}

.ac-section-rail-fill {
  display: block;
  height: 100%;
  transform-origin: left center;
  background: var(--sec-chrome);
  transition: transform 0.4s cubic-bezier(0.16, 1, 0.3, 1);
}

/* Assignment-gated and full screen. Without a way out the only exit is the
   browser's back button, which is a trap rather than a design. */
.ac-section-exit {
  border: none;
  border-radius: 9999px;
  background: none;
  padding: 0.25rem 0.75rem;
  color: inherit;
  font: inherit;
  cursor: pointer;
  transition: background-color 0.15s ease, color 0.15s ease;
}

.ac-section-exit:hover {
  background: #f1f5f9;
  color: var(--sec-ink);
}

/* ---- the paper --------------------------------------------------------- */

.ac-section-field {
  display: flex;
  flex: 1;
 * in lighter type so the elapsed figure is the one that reads first.
 */
.ac-section-clock {
  font-family: var(--sec-sans);
  font-size: 13px;
  font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums;
  color: var(--sec-chrome);
  white-space: nowrap;
}

.ac-section-clock i {
  font-style: normal;
  opacity: 0.55;
}

/* "Recording", in the rail, for the whole run.
 *
 * The one thing she asked to be able to see mid-section: that it is on. A
 * word and a dot, in the chrome rather than on the paper, so it is always in
 * the corner of her eye and never near the text. The word carries the meaning;
 * the red is on top of it. */
.ac-section-rec {
  display: inline-flex;
  align-items: center;
  gap: 0.4rem;
  font-family: var(--sec-sans);
  font-size: 0.75rem;
  letter-spacing: 0.08em;
  text-transform: uppercase;
  color: var(--sec-ink);
  white-space: nowrap;
}
.ac-section-rec--off {
  width: 0;
}
.ac-section-rec i {
  width: 0.5rem;
  height: 0.5rem;
  border-radius: 50%;
  background: oklch(0.58 0.2 27);
  animation: ac-section-rec-pulse 1.6s ease-in-out infinite;
}
@keyframes ac-section-rec-pulse {
  0%,
  100% {
    opacity: 1;
  }
  50% {
    opacity: 0.35;
  }
}

/* Three seconds between pressing Start and the first stimulus.
 *
 * The microphone opens on mount and the clock starts with it, so without this
 * the first thing a 25-minute recorded take captures is a student still
 * settling. It also puts the one instruction she needs -- Space, twice per
 * stimulus -- on screen at the moment it becomes true. */
.ac-section-ready {
  display: flex;
  flex: 1;
  flex-direction: column;
  align-items: center;
  justify-content: center;
  gap: 1.25rem;
  padding: 3rem 1.5rem;
  text-align: center;
}

.ac-section-ready-title {
  margin: 0;
  color: var(--sec-ink);
  font-family: Fraunces, ui-serif, Georgia, serif;
  font-size: 26px;
  font-weight: 400;
  font-optical-sizing: none;
  font-variation-settings: "opsz" 14;
}

.ac-section-ready-body {
  max-width: 34ch;
  margin: 0;
  color: #64748b;
  font-size: 0.875rem;
  line-height: 1.65;
}

.ac-section-ready-count {
  color: #94a3b8;
  font-size: 0.8125rem;
  font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums;
}

/* The refused microphone reads as a fault rather than as a beat, so the heading
   takes the warning colour the footer uses for the same class of thing. */
.ac-section-ready[data-mic="refused"] .ac-section-ready-title {
  color: var(--sec-warn);
}

/* Two ways forward, neither of which existed. Asking again leads, because it is
   the one that keeps the section. */
.ac-section-ready-actions {
  display: flex;
  align-items: center;
  gap: 0.75rem;
  flex-wrap: wrap;
  justify-content: center;
}

.ac-section-ready-retry,
.ac-section-ready-leave {
  border-radius: 9999px;
  padding: 0.4rem 1.1rem;
  font: inherit;
  font-size: 0.8125rem;
  cursor: pointer;
  transition: background-color 0.15s ease, color 0.15s ease, border-color 0.15s ease;
}

.ac-section-ready-retry {
  border: 1px solid var(--sec-ink);
  background: var(--sec-ink);
  color: var(--sec-paper);
}

.ac-section-ready-retry:hover {
  background: var(--sec-chrome);
  border-color: var(--sec-chrome);
}

.ac-section-ready-leave {
  border: 1px solid var(--sec-rule);
  background: none;
  color: var(--sec-chrome);
}

.ac-section-ready-leave:hover {
  background: #f1f5f9;
  color: var(--sec-ink);
}

import { readFileSync } from "node:fs";
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";

/*
 * The footer carries the instruction, the presence bar and the microphone
 * error. On a 13-inch laptop it was 55px below the fold, because the card
 * asked for 700px of height inside a 664px viewport, so the one line that says
 * "Nothing is reaching the microphone" was on a part of the page she had no
 * reason to scroll to during a timed run.
 *
 * jsdom does no layout, so the rule itself is what is asserted. The invariant
 * is the same either way: the card's floor must be bounded by the viewport, and
 * the footer must not be the thing that gives way when it is.
 */

const css = readFileSync("src/features/activity-centre/recall/section.css", "utf8");

function block(selector: string): string {
  const at = css.indexOf(`\n${selector} {`);
  expect(at, `${selector} is not declared at the top level`).toBeGreaterThan(-1);
  return css.slice(at, css.indexOf("}", at));
}

describe("the section card at a short viewport", () => {
  it("bounds the card's floor by the viewport rather than a bare constant", () => {
    const paper = block(".ac-section-paper");
    expect(paper).toMatch(/min-height:\s*min\(/);
    expect(paper).toMatch(/100vh/);
    expect(paper).not.toMatch(/min-height:\s*\d+px\s*;/);
  });

  it("never lets the footer be the thing that shrinks", () => {
    expect(block(".ac-section-foot")).toMatch(/flex-shrink:\s*0/);
  });

  it("lets the reading area give way instead, and scroll rather than clip", () => {
    const body = block(".ac-section-body");
    expect(body).toMatch(/min-height:\s*0/);
    expect(body).toMatch(/overflow-y:\s*auto/);
  });

  it("answers a short viewport with a height query, not only a width one", () => {
    expect(css).toMatch(/@media\s*\(max-height:/);
  });
});

import { useEffect, useRef } from "react";
import type { SectionPhase } from "./section";

/**
 * One quiet element that moves with her voice, and the only evidence on screen
 * that the microphone is alive.
 *
 * For twenty-one minutes she talks into a blank card. The recorder already
 * reports a REFUSED microphone, but it cannot see a live-but-silent one -- a
 * muted input, a device the OS switched underneath her, a headset that dropped
 * off. That failure produces a perfect-looking run, a clean upload, and a
 * Report Card of zeros, and she finds out at the end of the twenty-one minutes.
 *
 * So this does two jobs with one reading.
 *
 * **Presence.** A bar that answers "is anything reaching it" without answering
 * "how am I doing". Deliberately not a level meter with a target: nothing here
 * may look like a score, because nothing is judged until the section ends.
 *
 * **The fault.** If the input never leaves the noise floor across a whole
 * stimulus, that is said out loud at the boundary, while there is still a
 * section left to save -- and unsaid the moment anything is heard again.
 *
 * Nothing here re-renders the runner. The bar is written to as a DOM node
 * inside an animation frame, because a component that set state at 60Hz would
 * be re-rendering the drill room underneath her while she reads.
 */

/**
 * The line between "an open microphone in a quiet room" and "nothing arriving".
 *
 * RMS over the raw time-domain samples, so it is amplitude and not loudness. A
 * genuinely dead input sits at or near zero; a live one in a silent room still
 * carries the room. Set low on purpose: this exists to catch nothing at all,
 * never to judge a quiet speaker, and a false "your microphone is dead" during
 * a real run is worse than the fault it reports.
 */
const NOISE_FLOOR = 0.004;

/**
 * How long a phase has to run before its silence means anything.
 *
 * A Recall she ends in three seconds carries no evidence either way, and
 * accusing the hardware on that is how a warning stops being believed. A window
 * shorter than this counts neither for the fault nor against it.
 */
const MIN_WINDOW_MS = 8000;

/**
 * Consecutive silent windows before the fault is raised.
 *
 * Both halves of a stimulus are spoken -- "Read it. Tell it back and colour it in
 * as you go", then "Say the whole argument back from memory" -- so a dead input
 * is silent through every window there is. A skip is silent through exactly
 * one: she had nothing for that Recall, and then she reads the next stimulus
 * aloud like all the others.
 *
 * One window was therefore the wrong unit, and it accused the hardware on the
 * most ordinary thing a student does. Two consecutive is the shortest run a skip
 * cannot produce, and it costs a genuinely dead microphone one further stimulus
 * before it is named.
 */
const SILENT_WINDOWS = 2;

/** Full deflection. Chosen so ordinary speech uses most of the bar. */
const FULL_SCALE = 0.15;

export interface VoiceLevelProps {
  /** The live input, or null when the microphone never opened. */
  getStream: () => MediaStream | null;
  phase: SectionPhase;
  /**
   * Whether nothing is reaching the microphone, called only when the answer
   * changes.
   *
   * It used to be a one-way announcement, fired once and never taken back, and
   * the runner had no setter that could clear it. One deliberate skip therefore
   * told a student her hardware was broken, and went on telling her for the
   * remaining twenty-five stimuli and four minutes of clearly heard speech.
   */
...

import "fake-indexeddb/auto";
import { beforeEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from "vitest";
import { act, render } from "@testing-library/react";
import pt105 from "../content/read-recall-colour-pt105-s2.json";
import { SectionRunner } from "./SectionRunner";
import { discard, listPendingRuns } from "./recordingBackup";

/**
 * Leaving a section, and what must die with it.
 *
 * On 2026-09-07 a student opened a section at 23:28, left it, and came back at
 * 00:06 to run it properly. The first sitting's recorder was never stopped:
 * nothing stopped it on unmount, so it kept the microphone open and kept
 * writing its backup pieces for the next two hours, through her real run and
 * past the end of it. When she reloaded the page after finishing, that orphan
 * finally went quiet, the recovery pass took it for a crashed run, and
 * uploaded its 116-minute file OVER the 66-minute recording her real sitting
 * had just stored. The grader then refused the run: every window sat 37
 * minutes before its speech.
 *
 * Two rules, asserted here because both failures are silent. A recorder does
 * not outlive the component that started it, and a run she walked out of has
 * no backup worth recovering.
 */

type Row = Record<string, unknown>;
const attemptRows = { data: [] as Row[], error: null as unknown };
vi.mock("@/integrations/supabase/client", () => ({
  supabase: {
    from: () => ({
      upsert: () => Promise.resolve({ error: null }),
      update: () => ({ eq: () => Promise.resolve({ error: null }) }),
      select: () => ({
        eq: () => ({
          eq: () => ({ order: () => ({ limit: () => Promise.resolve(attemptRows) }) }),
        }),
      }),
    }),
    storage: { from: () => ({ upload: () => Promise.resolve({ error: null }) }) },
    functions: { invoke: () => Promise.resolve({ data: null, error: null }) },
  },
}));

class FakeRecorder {
  static isTypeSupported = () => true;
  static instances: FakeRecorder[] = [];
  ondataavailable: ((e: { data: Blob }) => void) | null = null;
  onstop: (() => void) | null = null;
  state = "inactive";
  constructor(
    _stream: MediaStream,
    readonly options?: { mimeType?: string },
  ) {
    FakeRecorder.instances.push(this);
  }
  get mimeType() {
    return this.options?.mimeType ?? "audio/webm";
  }
  start() {
    this.state = "recording";
  }
  stop() {
    this.state = "inactive";
    this.ondataavailable?.({ data: new Blob(["x"], { type: this.mimeType }) });
    this.onstop?.();
  }
}

const trackStop = vi.fn();
function grantMicrophone() {
  const stream = { getTracks: () => [{ stop: trackStop }] } as unknown as MediaStream;
  vi.stubGlobal("MediaRecorder", FakeRecorder);
  Object.defineProperty(navigator, "mediaDevices", {
    configurable: true,
    value: { getUserMedia: vi.fn(() => Promise.resolve(stream)) },
  });
}

const ONE = { ...pt105, items: [pt105.items[0]] };

async function mountRun() {
  const view = render(
    <SectionRunner
      assignmentId="a1"
      studentId="s1"
      items={ONE.items}
      sourceSection={ONE.source_section}
      timeGoalMinutes={ONE.time_goal_minutes}
      readySeconds={0}
    />,
  );
  // The microphone answers in a microtask; the Attempt lookup and the backup
  // announcement take a round trip each.
  await act(async () => {});
  await act(async () => {});
  await act(async () => {});
  return view;
}

beforeEach(async () => {
  grantMicrophone();
  FakeRecorder.instances = [];
  trackStop.mockClear();
  attemptRows.data = [];
  for (const run of await listPendingRuns()) await discard(run.runId);
});

describe("leaving a section before it ends", () => {
  it("stops every recorder and releases the microphone", async () => {
    const view = await mountRun();
    // The real recorder and its backup copy, both live.
    expect(FakeRecorder.instances.length).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(1);
    expect(FakeRecorder.instances.every((r) => r.state === "recording")).toBe(true);

    view.unmount();

    expect(FakeRecorder.instances.every((r) => r.state === "inactive")).toBe(true);
    expect(trackStop).toHaveBeenCalled();
  });

  it("throws the abandoned run's backup away, so recovery can never upload it", async () => {
    const view = await mountRun();
    expect((await listPendingRuns()).length).toBe(1);

    view.unmount();

    // The discard is an IndexedDB transaction, which settles on its own clock.
    await vi.waitFor(async () => expect((await listPendingRuns()).map((r) => r.runId)).toEqual([]), { timeout: 4000 });
  });
});

import { describe, expect, it, vi, beforeEach } from "vitest";
import { act, fireEvent, render, screen } from "@testing-library/react";
import pt105 from "../content/read-recall-colour-pt105-s2.json";
import pt134 from "../content/read-recall-colour-pt134-s3.json";
import { SectionRunner } from "./SectionRunner";
import { isRunLive } from "@/lib/liveRun";
import { stashRows } from "./recordingBackup";

/** The local stash a row that never landed goes into, so it has a way back. */
vi.mock("./recordingBackup", async (importOriginal) => {
  const real = await importOriginal<typeof import("./recordingBackup")>();
  return { ...real, stashRows: vi.fn(() => Promise.resolve()) };
});

// The section runner, driven the way she drives it: one key, pressed twice per
// stimulus, start to finish.
//
// Everything asserted here is a rule the design states negatively, and each one
// fails silently rather than loudly if it breaks: a stimulus that stays on
// screen through its Recall, a verdict shown mid-run, or a write inside the
// loop all look like a working screen. She runs twenty unbroken minutes with
// nothing checking on her, so these are asserted rather than trusted.

type Row = Record<string, unknown>;
/** Every write of a stimulus row: during the run, and the backstop at the end. */
const upsert = vi.fn((_rows: Row[]) => Promise.resolve({ error: null }));
/** The one read: which Attempt is this. */
const attemptRows = { data: [] as Row[], error: null as unknown };
const upload = vi.fn(() => Promise.resolve({ error: null }));
/** The assignment close: `.update(patch).eq("id", id)`. */
const update = vi.fn((_patch: Row) => Promise.resolve({ error: null }));
/** The fire-and-forget ask for a grade. */
const invoke = vi.fn((_fn: string, _opts: Row) => Promise.resolve({ data: null, error: null }));
vi.mock("@/integrations/supabase/client", () => ({
  supabase: {
    from: () => ({
      upsert: (rows: Row[]) => upsert(rows),
      update: (patch: Row) => ({ eq: () => update(patch) }),
      select: () => ({
        eq: () => ({
          eq: () => ({ order: () => ({ limit: () => Promise.resolve(attemptRows) }) }),
        }),
      }),
    }),
    storage: { from: () => ({ upload: () => upload() }) },
    functions: { invoke: (fn: string, opts: Row) => invoke(fn, opts) },
  },
}));

/**
 * A microphone that works, because a section without one no longer runs.
 *
 * jsdom has neither `navigator.mediaDevices` nor `MediaRecorder`, so every test
 * in this file used to take the recorder's refused-microphone path -- which was
 * the wrong default even before it stopped working. The drill is spoken end to

This hook measured from its own\n * mount instead, and the two are not the same instant: recognition starts on\n * mount, the recorder starts when `getUserMedia` resolves, and on a first run\n * that is a permission prompt a student has to read and answer. isFinal: boolean };\n\nclass FakeRecognition {\n  static live: FakeRecognition | null = null;\n  lang = \"\";\n  continuous = false;\n  interimResults = false;\n  onresult: ((e: { resultIndex: number; results: { length: number } & Record<number, Result> }) => void) | null = null;\n  onend: (() => void) | null = null;\n  onerror: (() => void) | null = null;\n  start() {\n    FakeRecognition.live = this;\n  }\n  stop() {}\n  abort() {}\n  /** One settled result, as the engine delivers it. */\n  say(text: string, index = 0) {\n    const results = { length: index + 1 } as { length: number } & Record<number, Result>;\n    results[index] = { 0: { transcript: text }, isFinal: true };\n    this.onresult?.({ resultIndex: index, results });\n  }\n}\n\nlet clock = 0;\nconst now = () => clock;\n\nbeforeEach(() => {\n  clock = 0;\n  FakeRecognition.live = null;\n  vi.stubGlobal(\"SpeechRecognition\", FakeRecognition);\n});\n\ndescribe(\"what the transcript's offsets are measured from\", () => {\n  it(\"measures from the recording's start, not from its own\", () => {\n    // She spent eight seconds on the permission prompt, so the file begins\n    // eight seconds after recognition did.\n    let recordingStartedAt: number | null = null;\n    const { result } = renderHook(() =>\n      useSectionTranscript(true, now, () => recordingStartedAt),\n    );\n\n    clock = 8_000;\n    recordingStartedAt = 8_000;\n    clock = 9_500;\n    FakeRecognition.live?.say(\"the argument moves on the second sentence\");\n\n    // A second and a half into the FILE, which is where the first segment is.\n    // Measured from mount it would have been 9.5s, deep inside a later window.\n    expect(result.current.transcript()).toEqual([\n      { atMs: 1_500, text: \"the argument moves on the second sentence\" },\n    ]);\n  });\n\n  it(\"drops words spoken before the file existed rather than guessing at them\", () => {\n    // The ready beat asks her to say something to check the microphone. Those\n    // words are genuinely not in the recording, so there is no stretch of it\n    // they could be filed against.\n    const { result } = renderHook(() => useSectionTranscript(true, now, () => null));\n\n    clock = 500;\n    FakeRecognition.live?.say(\"testing\");\n\n    expect(result.current.transcript()).toEqual([]);\n  });\n\n  it(\"keeps one timeline across a session the engine ends on silence\", () => {\n    // Engines close a session after a stretch of quiet even with `continuous`\n    // set, and she is silent for every read in the section. Re-zeroing on the\n    // restart would time the rest of the run from the middle of it.\n    const { result } = renderHook(() => useSectionTranscript(true, now, () => 0));\n\n    clock = 2_000;\n    FakeRecognition.live?.say(\"first\");\n\n    clock = 400_000;\n    FakeRecognition.live?.onend?.();\n    clock = 401_000;\n    FakeRecognition.live?.say(\"after the restart\");\n\n    expect(result.current.transcript()).toEqual([\n      { atMs: 2_000, text: \"first\" },\n      { atMs: 401_000, text: \"after the restart\" },\n    ]);\n  });\n});\n"}

 *
 * Not a fifth band, because it is not a worse score -- a recital can carry every
 * detail, and that is exactly the problem. It is the statement that the number
 * beside it did not come from memory, so it takes no colour at all: the point is
 * the method, not a mark against her.
 */
const RECITED = { label: "Said back word for word", mark: "recited" };

function minutes(seconds: number): string {
  const m = Math.floor(seconds / 60);
  const s = Math.round(seconds % 60);
  return `${m}:${String(s).padStart(2, "0")}`;
}

/**
 * Play one stimulus's stretch of the section recording, and say where it is.
 *
 * The whole run is one file and the trial proved a seek into it lands with zero
 * error, so a Clip is a start offset, an end offset and one audio element.
 * Nothing is cut server-side and nothing is downloaded twice.
 *
 * `positionMs` is the addition that makes the transcript follow the sound. The
 * grader already stores a start and an end for each line she spoke, so the card
 * does not need word timings to know which line is in the air: it needs the
 * playhead, and that is one number ticking off the element it already owns.

            <div className="ac-section-paper">
              <div className="ac-section-body">
                <p className="ac-section-finished">
                  Section finished.
                  <span>{run.stimuli.length} stimuli, end to end. Your Report Card comes next.</span>
                </p>
              </div>
            </div>
          </main>
        </div>
      ) : (
      <SectionStage
        stimulus={stimulus}
        phase={cursor.phase}
        stimulusNumber={cursor.item + 1}
        totalStimuli={run.stimuli.length}
        elapsedSeconds={beat ? 0 : 754}
        countdown={beat ? 3 : null}
        // No microphone in the lab, so the bar mounts and stays flat. That is
        // exactly what a student with a dead input sees, which is the reason
        // the indicator exists and the one state worth being able to look at.
        getStream={() => null}
        onExit={() => setConfirming(true)}
        confirmingExit={confirming}
        onConfirmExit={() => setConfirming(false)}
        onCancelExit={() => setConfirming(false)}
      />
      )}
    </div>
  );
}

/*
 * The standard's own recordings, judged by the judge that is live.
 *
 *   npx vite-node scripts/rrc-census.ts                    # the three ADR 0018 sections
 *   npx vite-node scripts/rrc-census.ts -- --all           # every built section
 *   npx vite-node scripts/rrc-census.ts -- --section pt102-s3
 *
 * ADR 0018 set the top band at the 10th percentile of Joshua's 71 model runs
 * and validated the judge against them on gemini-2.5-flash. When the judge's
 * model changes the scale moves with it, and the shaped transcripts and the
 * key that validation used live on a machine this one is not. So the census
 * runs INSIDE `grade-rrc-section` (admin-only `{ census }` call): same prompt,
 * same read-stripping, same model, same key as a student's grade. This script
 * only sends the authored items and prints the distribution.
 *
 * Reads as the admin account. Run it from the repo root. Nothing is written
 * to the database; the rows are saved under docs/adr/ for the ADR that cites
 * them.
 */
import fs from "node:fs";
import path from "node:path";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
import { supabase } from "@/integrations/supabase/client";

const REPO = path.resolve(path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), "..");
const CONTENT = path.join(REPO, "src/features/activity-centre/content");
const CENSUS_SECTIONS = ["pt102-s3", "pt105-s2", "pt134-s3"];

  const out: Record<string, string> = {};

  const out: Record<string, string> = {};
  if (!fs.existsSync(file)) return out;
  for (const line of fs.readFileSync(file, "utf8").split(/\r?\n/)) {
    const t = line.trim();
    if (!t || t.startsWith("#")) continue;
    const i = t.indexOf("=");
    if (i < 0) continue;
    out[t.slice(0, i).trim()] = t.slice(i + 1).trim().replace(/^["']|["']$/g, "");
  }
  return out;
}

function arg(name: string): string | null {
  const i = process.argv.indexOf(`--${name}`);
  return i === -1 ? null : (process.argv[i + 1] ?? "");
}

function percentile(sorted: number[], p: number): number {
  if (!sorted.length) return NaN;
  const idx = (sorted.length - 1) * p;
  const lo = Math.floor(idx);
  const hi = Math.ceil(idx);
  return Math.round(sorted[lo] + (sorted[hi] - sorted[lo]) * (idx - lo));
}

/*
 * Every finished Read, Recall, Colour section that has no complete Report Card.
 *
 *   npx vite-node scripts/rrc-ungraded.ts            # list
 *   npx vite-node scripts/rrc-ungraded.ts -- --grade # list, then re-ask the grader for each
 *
 * The sweeper (`sweep-rrc-grading`) is deliberately scheduled nowhere (ADR
 * 0021), the Report Card asks for a grade twice and then says "still being
 * marked" for good, and a run the grader REFUSES -- no recording, a clock it
 * cannot place, a transcription outage -- produces nothing anywhere that
 * Joshua would see. On 2026-09-08 a student sat on "still being marked" for
 * three and a half hours before she messaged. This is the list she was on.
 *
 * Reads as the admin account. Run it from the repo root.
 */
import fs from "node:fs";
import path from "node:path";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
import { supabase } from "@/integrations/supabase/client";

const REPO = path.resolve(path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), "..");
/** Younger than this and the grader is probably still running. */
const SETTLE_MS = 10 * 60 * 1000;

function readEnv(file: string): Record<string, string> {
  const out: Record<string, string> = {};
  if (!fs.existsSync(file)) return out;
  for (const line of fs.readFileSync(file, "utf8").split(/\r?\n/)) {
    const t = line.trim();
    if (!t || t.startsWith("#")) continue;
    const i = t.indexOf("=");
    if (i < 0) continue;
    out[t.slice(0, i).trim()] = t.slice(i + 1).trim().replace(/^["']|["']$/g, "");
  }
  return out;
}

async function main() {
  const env = { ...readEnv(path.resolve(REPO, "../../.env")), ...readEnv(path.join(REPO, ".env")) };
  const grade = process.argv.includes("--grade");
  const { error: authErr } = await supabase.auth.signInWithPassword({
    email: env.LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL,
    password: [REDACTED],
  });
  if (authErr) throw authErr;

  const { data: runs, error } = await supabase
    .from("activity_assignments")
    .select("id, student_id, title, source_section, status, started_at, completed_at")
    .eq("mode", "section")
    .eq("status", "completed")
    .order("completed_at", { ascending: false })
    .limit(200);
  if (error) throw error;

  const ids = (runs ?? []).map((r) => r.id);
  const { data: grades, error: gErr } = await supabase
    .from("activity_section_grades")
    .select("assignment_id, attempt_no, graded_at, section")
    .in("assignment_id", ids.length ? ids : ["-"]);
  if (gErr) throw gErr;
  const { data: rows, error: rErr } = await supabase
    .from("activity_item_responses")
    .select("assignment_id, attempt_no")
    .in("assignment_id", ids.length ? ids : ["-"]);
  if (rErr) throw rErr;

  const latestAttempt = new Map<string, number>();
  for (const r of rows ?? []) {
    latestAttempt.set(r.assignment_id, Math.max(latestAttempt.get(r.assignment_id) ?? 0, r.attempt_no ?? 1));
  }
  const gradeFor = new Map<string, { complete: unknown; graded_at: string }>();
  for (const g of grades ?? []) {
    gradeFor.set(`${g.assignment_id}:${g.attempt_no}`, {
      complete: (g.section as Record<string, unknown> | null)?.complete,
      graded_at: g.graded_at,
    });
  }

  // Names come from the admin roster, the same way `student-work.ts` gets them:

/** Silence inside The Recall, in seconds, before it counts as dead air. */
const DEAD_AIR_SECONDS = 4;

/**
 * How many stimuli are judged at once.
 *
 * A section is 22 of them. Judged in series they took longer than the edge
 * function is allowed to run, and the whole grade was lost at the end of a
 * correct recording, a correct upload and a correct transcription. Judged at
 * this width the judging stage costs about as long as its slowest single call.
 *
 * Not unbounded: the judge is a rate-limited API, and 429s would trade a
 * timeout for a different failure with the same result.
 */
const JUDGE_CONCURRENCY = 8;

interface Word { word: string; start: number; end: number; punctuated?: string }
interface Segment { slug: string; order: number; phase: string; startMs: number; endMs: number }

/** Constant-time string compare, so a secret is not probed a byte at a time. */
function sameSecret(expected: string, actual: string): boolean {
  if (expected.length !== actual.length) return false;
  let difference = 0;
  for (let i = 0; i < expected.length; i++) difference |= expected.charCodeAt(i) ^ actual.charCodeAt(i);
  return difference === 0;
}

function json(body: unknown, status = 200) {
  return new Response(JSON.stringify(body), {
    status,
    headers: { ...corsHeaders, "Content-Type": "application/json" },
  });
}

/**
 * Transcribe the recording Deepgram can fetch at `audioUrl`.
 *
 * By URL, never by bytes. This function used to download the recording and
 * post the buffer, which held the whole file in an edge function's memory
 * twice over. A 20-minute section fit; a 66-minute one (112 MB, Celia,
 * 2026-09-08) did not, and the function died with WORKER_RESOURCE_LIMIT before
 * a single word was transcribed -- the one failure the sweeper cannot repair
 * by re-driving, because every re-drive dies the same way. Deepgram fetches
 * the file itself from a short-lived signed URL, so this function never holds
 * the audio and the length of a section stops being a limit on grading it.
 */
async function transcribe(audioUrl: string): Promise<Word[]> {
  const key = Deno.env.get("DEEPGRAM_API_KEY");
  if (!key) throw new Error("DEEPGRAM_API_KEY is not configured");
  const res = await fetch(
    "https://api.deepgram.com/v1/listen?model=nova-3&punctuate=true&smart_format=true",

Tool result:
-- A run is graded once, and an abandoned one never.
--
-- Two things the 2026-09-03 student-run audit found on the ordinary path.
--
-- 1. The grader ran twice on every run. The Report Card asks again twenty
--    seconds after the runner's own ask, the first grading takes a minute or
--    more, and nothing on record said one was in flight -- so both transcribed,
--    both judged, and the card she read was overwritten by a second verdict on
--    the same audio. The grader now claims an Attempt here before it starts and
--    releases it when it ends. A claim that was never released expires, so a
--    grader that died mid-run cannot hold its Attempt hostage.
--
-- 2. Starting a section again from the library marked the abandoned run as
--    completed, and a completed run with rows is exactly what the grader and
--    the sweeper grade. A run abandoned at stimulus 3 came back as a Report
--    Card reading "0%, 2 stimuli, inside the time". ADR 0023 says an abandoned
--    run is never graded; it now has a status that says so.

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- 1. The claim.
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

create table if not exists public.activity_section_grading_claims (
  assignment_id uuid not null references public.activity_assignments(id) on delete cascade,
  attempt_no smallint not null,
  claimed_at timestamptz not null default now(),
  primary key (assignment_id, attempt_no)
);

comment on table public.activity_section_grading_claims is
  'One row per Attempt whose grading is in flight. Written by the grader before it transcribes, removed when it finishes, and ignored once stale. Service role only.';

alter table public.activity_section_grading_claims enable row level security;
-- No policies: nothing but the service role reads or writes this.

-- Take the claim, or report that someone else holds it.
--
-- One statement, so two graders arriving together cannot both succeed: the
-- insert either lands or conflicts, and on conflict the row is only taken over
-- if its holder has been silent for longer than any grading takes.
create or replace function public.claim_section_grading(
  p_assignment_id uuid,
  p_attempt_no integer,
  p_stale_seconds integer default 600
)

## assistant

Done. Yahunathan was asking for a pause, and the data shows exactly what happened when he couldn't.

## What actually happened to his run

His run is `8c688375` (PT1.S3, 22 stimuli). Per-stimulus timings from the database:

| Stimulus | Time on it |
|---|---|
| 1–17 | 5s – 208s each (normal) |
| **18** | **5,568s — 1h 32m** |
| 19–22 | 98s – 131s each (normal) |

He hit stimulus 18, needed to stop, had no way to. The drill sat open with the clock and the recorder running for an hour and a half, then he came back and finished normally. Consequences:

- Total run **7,910s (2h 12m)** against a 25-minute goal.
- Recording: **127 MB** of webm, most of it an empty room.
- Stimulus 18's transcript holds **4,026 words** of whatever was said in that room, filed as his recall.
- **The run was never graded.** Zero rows in `activity_section_grades`, 4+ hours after he finished. He is sitting on "still being marked". The grader itself is healthy — I probed the deployed function: build `2026-09-08f`, judge `gemini-direct` OK, Deepgram present. The failure is almost certainly the edge function timing out on a 2h12m/127MB transcribe-plus-judge; the by-URL fix removed the memory limit, not the wall-clock one. Nothing retries it, because the sweeper is scheduled nowhere.

So the missing pause is not just a comfort feature — it is what produced a 127 MB file and a failed grade.

## What I built

**Pause, in `SectionStage` / `SectionRunner`.** A `Pause` button in the rail beside `Leave`. Pressing it:

- takes the stimulus **out of the DOM** (not blurred, not dimmed — same rule as The Recall, otherwise a stopped clock is unlimited free reading time)
- pauses the recorder rather than stopping it, so it stays one file
- freezes every clock in the run through one `runNow()` that all timings read — the rail clock, per-stimulus seconds, the recording index and the transcript. That single clock is the correctness argument: the file skips the break, so the timeline must too, or every segment after the first pause names audio minutes away from the words it claims
- shuts the loop, so Space can't advance from behind the card; `Back to it` takes focus, so the key under her thumb resumes
- stores `paused_seconds` and `pause_count` on the row, and surfaces them on the Report Card under Time ("7:00 paused across two breaks, which the time above does not include")

If a browser won't actually pause the recorder, it refuses to pause at all and says so, rather than half-pausing into misaligned data.

## Two other bugs found and fixed

1. **The transcript was measured from the wrong zero.** Recognition started at mount; the recorder started when `getUserMedia` resolved — a permission prompt apart on a first run. Every transcript piece was stamped that many seconds late and filed one segment further on than where it was said. Same failure `sliceTranscript` was rewritten to fix, arriving through the other door. It now shares the recorder's zero, and words spoken before the file exists are dropped rather than misfiled.

2. **The global Space handler ate spaces in textareas.** It `preventDefault`s on `document` and excluded only `BUTTON` and `INPUT` — not `TEXTAREA` or contenteditable. Worth noting: his message arrived as `Ineedtopausethe`. I could not reproduce the path that would put a textarea on screen during a run, so I'm not claiming this caused it — but the handler was wrong either way and is now fixed.

## Verified

- `npx tsc --noEmit` clean.
- Recall suite 250 pass (9 new pause tests, 3 new transcript-zero tests); activity-centre 2,711 pass.
- Full suite: 5 failures, all in `BookingOfferHub` / `SingleSession` — pre-existing booking failures, none in anything I touched.
- Drove the paused screen in the browser at `/activity-lab → Section → Paused`: stimulus absent from the DOM, level bar gone, `PAUSED` in the rail, footer reads "Paused. Nothing is being recorded.", focus on `Back to it`, resume restores everything, no console errors. Header stays one row at 375px.
- Not verified in a browser: the Report Card pause line (no graded run has `paused_seconds` yet).

## Two things needing your call

**Yahunathan's run.** One command re-drives the grade, but it will likely time out the same way on a 2h12m file:
```bash
npx vite-node scripts/rrc-ungraded.ts -- --grade
```
My read is it's better to tell him the run couldn't be marked and have him re-run it now that pause exists — but that's yours.

**I did not commit or push.** The working tree already had uncommitted work from another session — an `emailReportCard` feature in `grade-rrc-section`, plus classroom, `App.tsx`, `QuestionBankContext`, and `mcp/index.ts` changes. My grader edit sits on top of that unreleased email code, so deploying the function to get `paused_seconds` would also ship the email feature. The frontend pause works without any deploy; only the Report Card's pause line needs the function pushed.

  // Names come from the admin roster, the same way `student-work.ts` gets them:
  // `profiles` is not keyed by the auth id and is not readable this way.
  const who = new Map<string, string>();
  const { data: users } = await supabase.functions.invoke("admin-manage-users", { method: "GET" });
  for (const u of (users ?? []) as { class_id: string; display_name: string | null; email: string | null }[]) {
    who.set(u.class_id, u.display_name || u.email || u.class_id);
  }

  const now = Date.now();
  const stuck: { id: string; student: string; section: string; finished: string; reason: string; age: string }[] = [];
  for (const r of runs ?? []) {
    const attempt = latestAttempt.get(r.id) ?? 1;
    const g = gradeFor.get(`${r.id}:${attempt}`);
    const finishedAt = r.completed_at ?? r.started_at;
    const ageMs = finishedAt ? now - new Date(finishedAt).getTime() : 0;
    if (ageMs < SETTLE_MS) continue;
    let reason: string | null = null;
    if (!g) reason = latestAttempt.has(r.id) ? "never graded" : "no rows at all";
    else if (g.complete === false) reason = "graded partial";
    if (!reason) continue;
    stuck.push({
      id: r.id,
      student: who.get(r.student_id) ?? r.student_id,
      section: r.source_section ?? r.title ?? "?",
      finished: finishedAt ? finishedAt.slice(0, 16).replace("T", " ") : "?",
      reason,
      age: `${Math.round(ageMs / 3_600_000)}h`,
    });
  }

  if (!stuck.length) {
    console.log(`Every finished section older than ${SETTLE_MS / 60_000} minutes has a complete Report Card (${runs?.length ?? 0} checked).`);
    return;
  }
  console.log(`READ, RECALL, COLOUR — ${stuck.length} finished run(s) with no complete Report Card\n`);
  for (const s of stuck) {
    console.log(`  ${s.finished}Z  ${s.age.padStart(5)}  ${s.student.padEnd(22)} ${s.section.padEnd(10)} ${s.reason.padEnd(14)} ${s.id}`);
  }

  if (!grade) {
    console.log("\nRe-ask the grader for each with:  npx vite-node scripts/rrc-ungraded.ts -- --grade");
    return;
  }
  for (const s of stuck) {
    if (s.reason === "no rows at all") continue;
    const t0 = Date.now();
    const { data, error: fnErr } = await supabase.functions.invoke("grade-rrc-section", {
      body: { assignmentId: s.id },
    });
    let body = "";
    const ctx = (fnErr as { context?: { text?: () => Promise<string> } } | null)?.context;
    if (ctx && typeof ctx.text === "function") body = (await ctx.text()).slice(0, 300);
    console.log(
      `\n  ${s.student} ${s.section}: ${Math.round((Date.now() - t0) / 1000)}s → ${
        fnErr ? `${fnErr.message} ${body}` : JSON.stringify(data).slice(0, 300)
      }`,
    );
  }
}

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Safe push to origin/main when Lovable may have committed in parallel.
# Fetches, fast-forwards or merges (never rebases, never force-pushes), then pushes.

set -e
cd "$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)"

# Typecheck gate: tsc does not run during `vite build` (esbuild strips types), so a
# type error (e.g. a missing import that crashes at runtime) would otherwise ship to
# main and auto-deploy. Block the push on any compile error. Set SKIP_TYPECHECK=1 to
# bypass for an emergency push.
if [ "${SKIP_TYPECHECK:-0}" != "1" ]; then
  echo "[safe-push] Typechecking (npm run typecheck)..."
  if ! npm run --silent typecheck; then
    echo "[safe-push] Typecheck FAILED. Fix the errors above or push with SKIP_TYPECHECK=1." >&2
    exit 1
  fi
  echo "[safe-push] Typecheck passed."
fi

git fetch origin main --quiet

LOCAL=$(git rev-parse HEAD)
REMOTE=$(git rev-parse origin/main)
BASE=$(git merge-base HEAD origin/main)

if [ "$LOCAL" = "$REMOTE" ]; then
  echo "[safe-push] Already up to date with origin/main."
elif [ "$REMOTE" = "$BASE" ]; then
  echo "[safe-push] origin/main has no new commits. Pushing."
else
  echo "[safe-push] origin/main has diverged (Lovable likely committed). Merging."
  git merge origin/main --no-edit
fi

git push origin main
echo "[safe-push] Pushed $(git log -1 --format='%h %s')"

On 2026-09-08 a student sat at\nstimulus 18 of 22 and asked for the thing that did not exist. His run shows\nwhat happens without it: stimulus 18 took 5,568 seconds against 5-208 for\nevery other one, the section ran 2h12m against a 25 minute goal, and the\nrecording came to 127 MB of mostly empty room.\n\nPause. The stimulus comes OFF the screen for the whole break, because a\npaused stimulus she can still read is untimed reading time on a timed drill.\nThe recorder pauses rather than stops, so it is still one file. Every clock\nin the run reads one paused-aware `runNow`, which is the correctness\nargument rather than tidiness: the file skips the break, so the index and\nthe transcript must skip it by the same arithmetic or every segment after a\npause names audio minutes away from the words it claims. A browser that will\nnot really pause the recorder is refused a pause and told why, rather than\nhalf-pausing into misaligned data. How long she was away is stored on the\nrow and shown under Time on the Report Card, so a broken sitting cannot read\nas an unbroken one.\n\nTwo bugs found on the way, both silent:\n\n- The transcript measured its offsets from its own mount while the segments\n  measured from the recording's start, and those are a permission prompt\n  apart on a first run. Every piece was stamped that late and filed one\n  segment on from where it was said -- the failure `sliceTranscript` was\n  rewritten to fix, arriving by the other door. It now shares the recorder's\n  zero, and words spoken before the file exists are dropped rather than\n  misfiled.\n\n- The document-level Space handler called preventDefault and excluded only\n  BUTTON and INPUT, so every TEXTAREA and contenteditable on screen during a\n  section silently lost its spaces.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>\nMSGEOF\ngit log -1 --format='%h %s'", "description": "Commit the pause work"}

## assistant

Both done.

## His run is saved — no redo

It graded itself at **04:39:12Z**, while I was working. Nothing I did triggered it; most likely he reopened the Report Card, which polls and re-asks. So the two-hour run went through after all.

More importantly, I checked whether the card is *trustworthy* or an artefact of the 92-minute gap. It's trustworthy:

- `drift_ms: -3025` — the audio clock and the window boundaries disagree by **3 seconds across 2h12m**. Alignment is essentially perfect.
- `words_inside_a_window: 7563 / 7563` — Deepgram transcribed the whole file and every single word landed in a window. Nothing spilled or got dropped.
- `ungraded_count: 0` — the judge answered for all 22 stimuli.

So the grade is real work, not garbage. What it says:

| | |
|---|---|
| Mean coverage | 37% |
| Reads given | **0 of 22** |
| Uncoloured | **21 of 22** |
| Read aloud | 0 chunks, 0 words |
| Reciting | 0 (he isn't parroting) |
| Listing | 6 |

The one number that is genuinely meaningless is the clock: 7,910s against a 25-minute goal, `within_time: false`. That's the 92 minutes he was away, not slowness. Everything else stands.

The real finding is pedagogical, not technical: he's doing recall only. No colouring on 21 of 22, no Read on any of the 22, and nothing read aloud. He's holding roughly a third of the details from memory and skipping both things the drill exists to train. That's worth a conversation with him and it would have been invisible if the run had stayed ungraded.

**One offer, your call:** I could write `paused_seconds: 5478, pause_count: 1` onto stimulus 18's row, so the card reads "40m, not counting 1:31 paused" instead of "2h12m". It's arguably more honest than the current figure — but he didn't pause, he left it running, and I'm not going to edit a student's record to say otherwise without you saying so. It also wouldn't display until the edge function is deployed.

## Pushed to main

Two commits, now at `218020c` (safe-push merged an incoming Lovable commit for one-off booking links first):

- `d3c096f` — pause plus the two timing bugs
- `6b2fa12` — a narrowing fix the push gate caught that my earlier `npx tsc --noEmit` missed, because `npm run typecheck` uses `tsconfig.app.json`. TS had pinned `rec.state` to a literal and called the no-op-pause check impossible. The runtime check is the whole point of refusing a fake pause, so I widened the reads rather than deleting them. 253 recall tests pass after it.

**What I deliberately did not push.** `grade-rrc-section` also contained another session's unreleased `emailReportCard` feature — it auto-sends a student her Report Card. I committed only my two hunks from that file and left the email work in your working tree untouched (verified: the pushed version has zero references to it, your working copy still has both). Shipping an untested auto-emailer would break your draft-never-send rule, and it isn't mine to release.

Also still uncommitted from other sessions, untouched: `App.tsx`, the classroom files, `QuestionBankContext`, `DESIGN.md`, `package.json`, `supabase/config.toml`, `mcp/index.ts`.

**For your Lovable publish:** the pause is pure frontend and works on a `deploy_project` alone. The only piece that needs the edge function deployed separately is the "X paused across N breaks" line under Time on the Report Card — and deploying that function right now would also ship the email feature, so I'd hold it until you've decided about that.

The grade underneath it is\n * sound: the transcript drifted three seconds across the whole file and every\n * word landed inside a window. The recording\n *   still physically contains the break -- we did not cut the file -- so its\n *   index has to keep describing the file as it actually is. null : (process.argv[i + 1] ?? null);\n};\n\nasync function main() {\n  const env = { ...readEnv(path.resolve(REPO, \"../../.env\")), ...readEnv(path.join(REPO, \".env\")) };\n  const { error: authErr } = await supabase.auth.signInWithPassword({\n    email: env.LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL,\n    password: [REDACTED],\n  });\n  if (authErr) throw authErr;\n\n  const assignmentId = arg(\"assignment\");\n  const from = Number(arg(\"from\"));\n  const pausedSeconds = Number(arg(\"seconds\"));\n  const note = arg(\"note\") ?? \"\";\n  const apply = process.argv.includes(\"--apply\");\n  if (!assignmentId || !Number.isFinite(from) || !Number.isFinite(pausedSeconds)) {\n    throw new Error(\"need --assignment <id> --from <order> --seconds <n>\");\n  }\n\n  const { data: rows, error: rErr } = await supabase\n    .from(\"activity_item_responses\")\n    .select(\"id, item_order, attempt_no, rrc\")\n    .eq(\"assignment_id\", assignmentId)\n    .order(\"attempt_no\")\n    .order(\"item_order\");\n  if (rErr) throw rErr;\n  if (!rows?.length) throw new Error(\"no response rows for that assignment\");\n\n  const attempt = Math.max(...rows.map((r) => r.attempt_no ?? 1));\n  const mine = rows.filter((r) => (r.attempt_no ?? 1) === attempt);\n  const rawTotal = Math.max(\n    ...mine.map((r) => Number((r.rrc as Record<string, unknown>).finished_at_seconds ?? 0)),\n  );\n  const correctedTotal = rawTotal - pausedSeconds;\n  if (correctedTotal <= 0) throw new Error(\"the break cannot be longer than the run\");\n\n  console.log(`attempt ${attempt}, ${mine.length} rows`);\n  console.log(`  raw clock        ${rawTotal}s (${(rawTotal / 60).toFixed(1)}m)`);\n  console.log(`  break            ${pausedSeconds}s (${(pausedSeconds / 60).toFixed(1)}m), from stimulus ${from}`);\n  console.log(`  corrected clock  ${correctedTotal}s (${(correctedTotal / 60).toFixed(1)}m)`);\n  console.log(`  ${apply ? \"APPLYING\" : \"dry run — pass --apply to write\"}\\n`);\n\n  for (const row of mine) {\n    const rrc = { ...(row.rrc as Record<string, unknown>) };\n    /*\n     * The corrected clock goes on every row, because the grader reads it from\n     * \"whichever row carries it\" and otherwise falls back to the last\n     * stimulus's raw finishing time -- which is the number being corrected.\n     */\n    rrc.section_total_seconds = correctedTotal;\n    // The break itself is cumulative, exactly as a real pause would have\n    // written it: absent from the rows that finished before it started.\n    if ((row.item_order ?? 0) >= from) {\n      rrc.paused_seconds = pausedSeconds;\n      rrc.pause_count = 1;\n      rrc.paused_reconstructed = {\n        why: \"Pausing did not exist when this section was run.\",\n        measured: note || null,\n        applied_at: new Date().toISOString(),\n        raw_section_total_seconds: rawTotal,\n      };\n    }\n    const marked = (row.item_order ?? 0) >= from ? \"  ← break\" : \"\";\n    console.log(\n      `  stimulus ${String(row.item_order).padStart(2)}  total=${correctedTotal}s  paused=${rrc.paused_seconds ?? 0}s${marked}`,\n    );\n    if (!apply) continue;\n    const { error: uErr } = await supabase\n      .from(\"activity_item_responses\")\n      .update({ rrc })\n      .eq(\"id\", row.id);\n    if (uErr) throw new Error(`row ${row.item_order}: ${uErr.message}`);\n  }\n\n  if (!apply) return;\n  console.log(\"\\nrows written.
